{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "d01b86df-99ac-4df2-af04-845b61c8cab8",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "c1b27e13-3736-4dc1-9b0e-f13823585f46",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "7bbadc5f-be58-4071-aad6-869e8d1ac10c",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "oldString": "More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought~\\citep{tot-2023}, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling for the tested classes, not a universal bound.",
    "newString": "More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}\nThe router diagnostic (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:ablations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}\nWhile death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three properties were not predictable a priori: (i)~\\emph{routing prior}---the oracle matches always-deterministic on 96.3\\% of decisions (2.89-point improvement), and threshold routing with perfect death risk knowledge still cannot outperform always-deterministic (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}), so the rational prior is ``do not route''; (ii)~\\emph{intervention resistance}---retry ($43.5\\%$), Reflexion ($34.0\\%$), $10{\\times}$ scaling ($30.6\\%$), and action-space filtering ($29.0\\%$) all leave ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety ($16.3\\%$), with Reflexion and scaling converging to overlapping Wilson CIs suggesting a shared bound from lacking ground-truth state access; (iii)~\\emph{cross-model stability}---Qwen-7B and Llama-8B show nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\\sim}$42\\%), and 72B scaling leaves death dominance intact (DAF $= 92.4\\%$). More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought~\\citep{tot-2023}, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling for the tested classes, not a universal bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}\nAll 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to misuse of \\texttt{focus on [object]}, the answer-submission action, as a general interaction command (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}). The root cause is grounding failure: agents parse task descriptions correctly but cannot map understanding to valid primitives; the majority of death episodes show effective reasoning in non-fatal steps before a single grounding error triggers irreversible failure. The internal strategy's higher death count (105 vs.\\ 40) reflects more frequent entry into catastrophic states, not different within-episode behavior (${\\sim}5\\%$ \\texttt{focus on} rate in both); lethality depends jointly on misuse rate and answer distribution (\\texttt{find-non-living-thing} has zero deaths despite high submission rate because non-living objects are the majority class). Direct grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate vulnerability ($48.0\\% \\to 29.0\\%$; Appendix~\\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nA three-stage diagnostic (${\\sim}$50-episode pilot $\\to$ death-rate differential $\\to$ death-label fraction; Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}) correctly predicts outcomes for all four conditions (Qwen-7B, Llama-8B, Qwen-72B, MATH). Our router training consumed ${\\sim}$200 GPU-hours without exceeding always-deterministic; this diagnostic could have predicted that from a 50-episode pilot. The 4B router's end-to-end score ($-12.03$, below always-deterministic $-9.47$) may partly reflect distribution shift at deployment~\\citep{dagger-2011}.\n\n\\paragraph{Framework applicability.}\nThe penalty-independent label fraction (78.9\\%) is the primary diagnostic; DAF increases monotonically with $P$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}). Preliminary pilots on MiniHack~\\citep{minihack-2021} (0\\% death) and Crafter~\\citep{crafter-2022} (100\\% death, both strategies) produced no death asymmetry, illustrating two boundary conditions: no death mechanism yields no signal; universal death lacks exploitable asymmetry. NetHack~\\citep{nethack-2020} combat tasks remain the most direct replication target \\citep{balrog-2025,netplay-2024}.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 5,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 5,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " The router diagnostic (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:ablations}).",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}",
          "-While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three properties were not predictable a priori: (i)~\\emph{routing prior}---the oracle matches always-deterministic on 96.3\\% of decisions (2.89-point improvement), and threshold routing with perfect death risk knowledge still cannot outperform always-deterministic (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}), so the rational prior is ``do not route''; (ii)~\\emph{intervention resistance}---retry ($43.5\\%$), Reflexion ($34.0\\%$), $10{\\times}$ scaling ($30.6\\%$), and action-space filtering ($29.0\\%$) all leave ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety ($16.3\\%$), with Reflexion and scaling converging to overlapping Wilson CIs suggesting a shared bound from lacking ground-truth state access; (iii)~\\emph{cross-model stability}---Qwen-7B and Llama-8B show nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\\sim}$42\\%), and 72B scaling leaves death dominance intact (DAF $= 92.4\\%$). More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought~\\citep{tot-2023}, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling for the tested classes, not a universal bound.",
          "+While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three properties were not predictable a priori: (i)~\\emph{routing prior}---the oracle matches always-deterministic on 96.3\\% of decisions (2.89-point improvement), and threshold routing with perfect death risk knowledge still cannot outperform always-deterministic (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}), so the rational prior is ``do not route''; (ii)~\\emph{intervention resistance}---retry ($43.5\\%$), Reflexion ($34.0\\%$), $10{\\times}$ scaling ($30.6\\%$), and action-space filtering ($29.0\\%$) all leave ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety ($16.3\\%$), with Reflexion and scaling converging to overlapping Wilson CIs suggesting a shared bound from lacking ground-truth state access; (iii)~\\emph{cross-model stability}---Qwen-7B and Llama-8B show nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\\sim}$42\\%), and 72B scaling leaves death dominance intact (DAF $= 92.4\\%$). More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}",
          " All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to misuse of \\texttt{focus on [object]}, the answer-submission action, as a general interaction command (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}). The root cause is grounding failure: agents parse task descriptions correctly but cannot map understanding to valid primitives; the majority of death episodes show effective reasoning in non-fatal steps before a single grounding error triggers irreversible failure. The internal strategy's higher death count (105 vs.\\ 40) reflects more frequent entry into catastrophic states, not different within-episode behavior (${\\sim}5\\%$ \\texttt{focus on} rate in both); lethality depends jointly on misuse rate and answer distribution (\\texttt{find-non-living-thing} has zero deaths despite high submission rate because non-living objects are the majority class). Direct grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate vulnerability ($48.0\\% \\to 29.0\\%$; Appendix~\\ref{app:grounding-ablation})."
        ]
      }
    ],

The issue is granularity mismatch---applying correct-direction labels to uninformative steps---not directional error.\"\n\n3. **method.tex §2 scope**（末尾段落）：\n   - \"of 2,040 training steps, only 0.3% exhibit strict directional conflict with episode labels, with 60.3% pre-event neutral (neither strategy advantaged). {
  "type": "queue-operation",
  "operation": "enqueue",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-19T16:10:46.759Z",
  "sessionId": "0523ef73-1599-429f-be17-206601cc9d71",
  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch — R19 综合修复批次，27 条，EMNLP 2026 DDL 2026-05-25]\n\n## 战略转向：Framework-centric Reframing (D126)\n\n核心变化：论文定位从 \"we found death avoidance dominates\" 转为 \"we develop a diagnostic framework; applying it to ScienceWorld reveals death avoidance dominates\"。标题保留（类似 \"Attention Is All You Need\" 用发现做标题但贡献是方法）。所有 section 需一致。\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix (5)\n\n### MF1 [R13-N1, 第6轮]: Abstract + §3.1 DAF headline → 78.9% primary\n\n**Abstract**：\n- 78.9% label fraction 作为唯一 headline 定量结果\n- DAF=90.4% 仅在从句中出现：\"a derived penalty-dependent measure (DAF) reaches 90.4% at P=100\"\n- CI 和 δ 值移到正文\n- 概念优先：先说框架做了什么、发现了什么，再给数字\n\n**§3.1**：\n- 第一段首句必须是 78.9% 结论：\"Of the 95 non-tie episodes, 75 (78.9%) receive their routing label from death asymmetry...\"\n- DAF 定义和数值放在第二段，明确标注 \"DAF is a derived, penalty-dependent quantity\"\n- 增加 DAF 边界行为：\"When Δ_nd → 0, DAF → 1 is algebraic necessity regardless of actual death dominance; conversely, when Δ_nd < 0 (as in Reflexion N≥3), DAF exceeds 100%, reflecting that death asymmetry more than accounts for the full gap.\"\n\n### MF2 [R14-W1/R16-N1]: §5 Non-triviality 重写\n\n三角度强化，每角度 2-3 句：\n\n**(a) Routing 文献 prior assumptions**：\n\"Existing routing work (Toolformer, CogRouter, ART, When2Tool, RouteLLM) implicitly assumes diverse routing signals—capability matching, efficiency, task complexity. No prior work has quantified signal composition. Our finding that a single mechanism (death avoidance) accounts for 78.9% of routing labels contradicts this implicit diversity assumption.\"\n\n**(b) Intervention resistance non-predictability**：\n\"From the setup alone (P=100, internal DR=42.9%), one might predict that scaling (72B), self-correction (Reflexion), or action filtering would substantially reduce death vulnerability. Instead, all four intervention axes fail to eliminate death dominance: 72B DAF=92.4%, Reflexion DAF>100% (Δ_nd=-0.47, meaning self-correction improves non-death performance while death dominance remains intact), action filtering reduces DR to 29% but ≥12pp residual gap persists. This resistance pattern is non-obvious from the experimental setup.\"\n\n**关键新数据点**：Reflexion DAF>100%（Δ_nd = -0.47）是最强 intervention resistance 证据——Reflexion 让 internal 在非死亡 episode 做得更好，但 death dominance 完整保留。\n\n**(c) Cross-model δ stability**：\n\"Qwen-7B (+26.5pp) and Llama-8B (+30.0pp) show nearly identical death-rate differentials despite different architectures and training corpora. Internal death rates converge to ~42% across both models. This structural consistency suggests death vulnerability is a property of the task-environment interaction, not model-specific weakness.\"\n\n**(d) 补一句 pragmatic value**：\n\"Even granting predictability in hindsight, precise quantification (78.9%, not 'most') enables principled deployment decisions—and the framework that produces this quantification is the primary contribution.\"\n\n### MF3 [R19-N1]: Framework-centric Reframing\n\n**Contributions 重组**（Introduction）：\n- C1: \"We propose an episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4% tie rate) in sparse-reward environments, enabling signal decomposition at the episode level.\"\n- C2: \"Applying this framework to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes), we find that 78.9% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry—a single mechanism dominates routing signal.\"\n- C3: \"Cross-model validation (Llama-3.1-8B), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the death-avoidance finding.\"\n\n**Abstract narrative**：framework → application → finding → validation → implication。Concept-first, numbers-second。\n\n**Conclusion**：围绕 framework applicability + case study implications + future diagnostic applications。\n\n### MF4 [R13-N2]: K=11 cluster bootstrap 处理\n\n§3.1 统计推断部分重组：\n- **主推断**：\"Non-clustered bootstrap (B=10,000) yields 95% CI [81.6%, 98.8%] for death-avoidance label fraction, independent of cluster assumptions.\"\n- **Sensitivity check**：\"As a conservative check, wild cluster bootstrap treating each task type as a cluster yields p=0.002. However, with K=11 clusters, this test falls below the K≥20-40 threshold recommended for reliable inference (Cameron et al., 2008; Webb, 2023 report size distortion at K<20). We therefore rely on the non-clustered bootstrap as primary inference.\"\n- **补充 robustness**：\"Leave-one-task-out analysis shows DAF ranges from 75.9% to 78.9%, and all 28 death-prone tasks share the same direction (Appendix F).\"\n- Cohen's d=3.02 加 caveat：\"should be interpreted cautiously given K=11\"\n\n### MF5 [R18-N1]: §3.6 Router 实验重构\n\n**Section 标题或首句明确**：\"Router Training: A Negative Result\"\n\n**两种解释并列**：\n\"The near-chance step-level accuracy admits two interpretations: (A) episode-level labels projected onto steps create ~88% uninformative signal, drowning learnable patterns in noise; (B) routing signal is genuinely task-type-level—there is no step-level pattern to learn. Evidence favors a mixture: directional conflict is only 0.3% (labels are directionally correct in 99.7% of informative steps), but 60.3% of steps are pre-event neutral (neither strategy advantaged). The label issue is granularity mismatch, not directional error.\"\n\n**Future work**：mention death-proximal labeling (training only on steps within k steps of death events) as promising direction.\n\n**Episode-level split**：报告 episode-level independent eval 数字在正文（47.1±2.2% < majority-vote 50.0%），不仅在 Appendix。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix (9)\n\n### SF1 [R15-N2]: §3.4 Cross-env → boundary probes\n\n§3.4 开头增加：\"We examine MATH and ALFWorld as boundary probes—not cross-domain validation. The three environments instantiate 'deterministic computation' differently: ScienceWorld uses an oracle simulator, MATH uses a code interpreter, and ALFWorld enumerates admissible actions. These probes test framework applicability boundaries, not finding generalizability.\"\n\nAbstract 相应措辞：从任何 \"confirmed across\" → \"probed against contrasting environments\"\n\n### SF2 [R19-W1]: P post-hoc scoring 声明\n\n§2.4 末尾（或 Appendix K 开头）增加：\n\"In ScienceWorld, P affects only the post-hoc score calculation; the agent's policy (ReAct prompting) does not include P in its observation. Death rates and Δ_nd are therefore P-invariant by design, and the sensitivity analysis in Table X [Appendix K] is exact rather than extrapolated.\"\n\n不需要实验。\n\n### SF3 [R17-W1]: Pure-strategy 估计\n\nLimitations 增加：\"Under mixed-strategy routing with oracle death prediction, switching to deterministic at the step before death could reduce internal death rate from 42.9%. Even under optimistic assumptions (perfect step-level death prediction), a residual gap of >10pp between strategies is expected, as fatal trajectories involve cumulative hallucination errors preceding the death event.\"\n\n### SF4 [R19-W2]: Conclusion 扩展 ~0.5 页\n\n围绕 framework-centric narrative：\n1. Key findings summary（3 句：framework enables signal decomposition → death avoidance dominates → robust across interventions）\n2. Diagnostic value：框架可提前判断 routing 是否值得投入，避免无效 router 训练投资\n3. Framework applicability：需要 episode-level evaluation + catastrophic failure 存在的环境\n4. Future directions：NetHack replication, step-level signal extraction (death-proximal labeling), mixed-strategy analysis, extension to multi-agent settings\n\n### SF5 [R13-W6]: §4 Safe RL 扩展 5+ 句\n\n扩展为：\n\"Death avoidance as an implicit routing signal connects to safe RL. Constrained MDPs (Altman, 1999) formalize safety through state or cost constraints; our death-avoidance mechanism can be viewed as an implicit absorbing-state constraint, where routing to deterministic computation avoids irreversible penalty states. Risk-sensitive RL (Mihatsch & Neuneier, 2002) optimizes CVaR-type objectives to minimize catastrophic outcomes—our framework diagnoses when and why such risk-sensitivity emerges in routing, rather than prescribing it. Unlike explicit safe RL methods that require constraint specification, our routing mechanism achieves safety implicitly through computation mode selection, without the agent being aware of the safety objective. Ruan et al. (2024) provide a taxonomy of LM agent failures identifying catastrophic actions as a distinct category, consistent with our finding that death events dominate routing signal.\"\n\n补 bib：altman1999constrained, mihatsch2002risk, natarajan2013learning（noisy label learning，与 MF5 §3.6 关联）\n\n### SF6 [R13-W5]: Abstract concept-first\n\n与 MF1 和 MF3 统一处理。Abstract 结构：\n- 句1-2：问题+框架（\"Per-step oracle comparison degenerates to ties in sparse-reward environments. We propose episode-level counterfactual diagnosis...\"）\n- 句3-4：key finding（\"Applying this framework to ScienceWorld reveals that 78.9% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry...\"）\n- 句5-6：validation（\"Cross-model replication, four intervention axes, and boundary probes confirm...\"）\n- 句7：implication（\"Our framework diagnoses when routing adds value...\"）\n- CI, δ, P sensitivity 全部移到正文\n\n### SF7 [R18-M1]: Modality → computation mode 验证\n\n全文搜索 \"modality\"（在 internal/deterministic 语境下）。R18 已做全局替换，确认是否遗漏。如有遗漏补上。\n\n### SF8 [R18-W2]: Reflexion power analysis（按 Reviewer 修正）\n\n§3.3 改为：\n\"The key decline (N=1→N=3, Δ=-13.5pp, z=2.75, p=0.003) is well-powered: with N=200 paired episodes, MDE ≈ 8pp at α=0.05, 80% power. The plateau claim (N=3→N=5, +1.0pp) is also supported: if a ≥8pp improvement existed, we would detect it; the observed +1.0pp falls within noise. We characterize this as suggestive evidence of a Reflexion ceiling rather than a conclusive demonstration.\"\n\n### SF9 [NEW]: §5 Routing Necessity 新段\n\n§5 新增 \"Practical Value of Routing\" 段（~4-5 句）：\n\"Our framework reveals a striking implication: 96.3% of oracle routing decisions favor always-deterministic, and 62% of episodes show no strategy preference. Routing, in its current form, is nearly unnecessary on ScienceWorld. This is not a limitation but a diagnostic finding—the framework's value lies precisely in making such assessments possible before investing in router training. Without counterfactual diagnosis, the default assumption (diverse routing signals exist) would lead to wasted computation on router architectures that cannot outperform the trivial always-deterministic baseline. The framework saves this investment by quantifying routing signal composition upfront.\"\n\n---\n\n## Minor (7)\n\n### m1 [R19-M1]: 62% tie rate\n\n被 SF9 段覆盖。如 SF9 段未充分讨论 tie rate 含义，在 §3.1 增加一句：\"150 of 245 episodes (62%) are ties, meaning routing is irrelevant for the majority; among the 95 non-tie episodes, 78.9% of labels stem from death asymmetry.\"\n\n### m2: Figure 2 Spearman's ρ\n\nFigure 2 caption 增加 \"(Spearman's ρ=0.975; Appendix D)\"\n\n### m3 [R17-M1]: Webb-2023\n\n确认为 2023 journal version：Webb, M. (2023). \"The Impact of Artificial Intelligence on the Labor Market.\" Canadian Journal of Economics, 56(3). 更新 bib。\n\n### m4: Appendix R thresholds\n\n增加：\"The f_death thresholds (50%, 75%) are illustrative decision boundaries; practitioners should calibrate to domain-specific risk tolerance and deployment constraints.\"\n\n### m5: AI disclosure\n\nResponsible NLP checklist 中 AI assistance 改为具体描述。\n\n### m6 [R19-M2]: Missing references\n\n与 SF5 合并处理。补：\n- altman1999constrained: Altman, E. (1999). Constrained Markov Decision Processes. Chapman & Hall/CRC.\n- mihatsch2002risk: Mihatsch, O. & Neuneier, R. (2002). Risk-sensitive reinforcement learning. Machine Learning, 49(2-3), 267-290.\n- natarajan2013learning: Natarajan, N. et al. (2013). Learning with Noisy Labels. NeurIPS 2013.\n\nNatarajan 在 §3.6 引用：\"The high uninformative rate connects to noisy-label learning theory (Natarajan et al., 2013): standard ERM on labels with ~88% noise/uninformative rate yields near-chance classifiers, consistent with our router results.\"\n\n### m7 [NEW]: Table 1 weighting\n\nTable 1 caption 增加：\"Episode-weighted mean across 245 episodes (11 task types, 20-40 episodes per type).\"\n\n---\n\n## 72B 数据修正（Reviewer 提醒）\n\nPaper 中 72B death rate 如仍有 31.4%，全部改为 30.6%（78/255=0.3059）。ΔDR 从 28.6pp 改为 27.8pp。请全文搜索确认。\n\n---\n\n## 页面预算\n\n以上修改增加内容较多（Conclusion +0.3页, §5 新增段 +0.2页, Safe RL 扩展 +0.1页）。需要从以下位置压缩：\n- §3.4 Cross-env probes 可压缩（降级为 boundary probes 后不需要详细讨论）\n- §1 Introduction 可适度压缩\n- Appendix 不计入页数限制\n\n目标：正文 ≤ 8 页，0 critical warnings。\n\n## 优先级\n\nMF2+MF3 优先（framework-centric 转向是最大的结构性变化，其他修改需要在此基础上保持一致）。"

## 战略转向：Framework-centric Reframing (D126)

核心变化：论文定位从 "we found death avoidance dominates" 转为 "we develop a diagnostic framework; applying it to ScienceWorld reveals death avoidance dominates"。标题保留（类似 "Attention Is All You Need" 用发现做标题但贡献是方法）。所有 section 需一致。

---

## Must-Fix (5)

### MF1 [R13-N1, 第6轮]: Abstract + §3.1 DAF headline → 78.9% primary

**Abstract**：
- 78.9% label fraction 作为唯一 headline 定量结果
- DAF=90.4% 仅在从句中出现："a derived penalty-dependent measure (DAF) reaches 90.4% at P=100"
- CI 和 δ 值移到正文
- 概念优先：先说框架做了什么、发现了什么，再给数字

**§3.1**：
- 第一段首句必须是 78.9% 结论："Of the 95 non-tie episodes, 75 (78.9%) receive their routing label from death asymmetry..."
- DAF 定义和数值放在第二段，明确标注 "DAF is a derived, penalty-dependent quantity"
- 增加 DAF 边界行为："When Δ_nd → 0, DAF → 1 is algebraic necessity regardless of actual death dominance; conversely, when Δ_nd < 0 (as in Reflexion N≥3), DAF exceeds 100%, reflecting that death asymmetry more than accounts for the full gap."

### MF2 [R14-W1/R16-N1]: §5 Non-triviality 重写

三角度强化，每角度 2-3 句：

**(a) Routing 文献 prior assumptions**：
"Existing routing work (Toolformer, CogRouter, ART, When2Tool, RouteLLM) implicitly assumes diverse routing signals—capability matching, efficiency, task complexity. No prior work has quantified signal composition. Our finding that a single mechanism (death avoidance) accounts for 78.9% of routing labels contradicts this implicit diversity assumption."

**(b) Intervention resistance non-predictability**：
"From the setup alone (P=100, internal DR=42.9%), one might predict that scaling (72B), self-correction (Reflexion), or action filtering would substantially reduce death vulnerability. Instead, all four intervention axes fail to eliminate death dominance: 72B DAF=92.4%, Reflexion DAF>100% (Δ_nd=-0.47, meaning self-correction improves non-death performance while death dominance remains intact), action filtering reduces DR to 29% but ≥12pp residual gap persists. This resistance pattern is non-obvious from the experimental setup."

**关键新数据点**：Reflexion DAF>100%（Δ_nd = -0.47）是最强 intervention resistance 证据——Reflexion 让 internal 在非死亡 episode 做得更好，但 death dominance 完整保留。

**(c) Cross-model δ stability**：
"Qwen-7B (+26.5pp) and Llama-8B (+30.0pp) show nearly identical death-rate differentials despite different architectures and training corpora. Internal death rates converge to ~42% across both models. This structural consistency suggests death vulnerability is a property of the task-environment interaction, not model-specific weakness."

**(d) 补一句 pragmatic value**：
"Even granting predictability in hindsight, precise quantification (78.9%, not 'most') enables principled deployment decisions—and the framework that produces this quantification is the primary contribution."

### MF3 [R19-N1]: Framework-centric Reframing

**Contributions 重组**（Introduction）：
- C1: "We propose an episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4% tie rate) in sparse-reward environments, enabling signal decomposition at the episode level."
- C2: "Applying this framework to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes), we find that 78.9% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry—a single mechanism dominates routing signal."
- C3: "Cross-model validation (Llama-3.1-8B), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the death-avoidance finding."

**Abstract narrative**：framework → application → finding → validation → implication。Concept-first, numbers-second。

**Conclusion**：围绕 framework applicability + case study implications + future diagnostic applications。

### MF4 [R13-N2]: K=11 cluster bootstrap 处理

§3.1 统计推断部分重组：
- **主推断**："Non-clustered bootstrap (B=10,000) yields 95% CI [81.6%, 98.8%] for death-avoidance label fraction, independent of cluster assumptions."
- **Sensitivity check**："As a conservative check, wild cluster bootstrap treating each task type as a cluster yields p=0.002. However, with K=11 clusters, this test falls below the K≥20-40 threshold recommended for reliable inference (Cameron et al., 2008; Webb, 2023 report size distortion at K<20). We therefore rely on the non-clustered bootstrap as primary inference."
- **补充 robustness**："Leave-one-task-out analysis shows DAF ranges from 75.9% to 78.9%, and all 28 death-prone tasks share the same direction (Appendix F)."
- Cohen's d=3.02 加 caveat："should be interpreted cautiously given K=11"

### MF5 [R18-N1]: §3.6 Router 实验重构

**Section 标题或首句明确**："Router Training: A Negative Result"

**两种解释并列**：
"The near-chance step-level accuracy admits two interpretations: (A) episode-level labels projected onto steps create ~88% uninformative signal, drowning learnable patterns in noise; (B) routing signal is genuinely task-type-level—there is no step-level pattern to learn. Evidence favors a mixture: directional conflict is only 0.3% (labels are directionally correct in 99.7% of informative steps), but 60.3% of steps are pre-event neutral (neither strategy advantaged). The label issue is granularity mismatch, not directional error."

**Future work**：mention death-proximal labeling (training only on steps within k steps of death events) as promising direction.

**Episode-level split**：报告 episode-level independent eval 数字在正文（47.1±2.2% < majority-vote 50.0%），不仅在 Appendix。

## Should-Fix (9)

### SF1 [R15-N2]: §3.4 Cross-env → boundary probes

§3.4 开头增加："We examine MATH and ALFWorld as boundary probes—not cross-domain validation. The three environments instantiate 'deterministic computation' differently: ScienceWorld uses an oracle simulator, MATH uses a code interpreter, and ALFWorld enumerates admissible actions. These probes test framework applicability boundaries, not finding generalizability."

Abstract 相应措辞：从任何 "confirmed across" → "probed against contrasting environments"

### SF2 [R19-W1]: P post-hoc scoring 声明

§2.4 末尾（或 Appendix K 开头）增加：
"In ScienceWorld, P affects only the post-hoc score calculation; the agent's policy (ReAct prompting) does not include P in its observation. Death rates and Δ_nd are therefore P-invariant by design, and the sensitivity analysis in Table X [Appendix K] is exact rather than extrapolated."

不需要实验。

### SF3 [R17-W1]: Pure-strategy 估计

Limitations 增加："Under mixed-strategy routing with oracle death prediction, switching to deterministic at the step before death could reduce internal death rate from 42.9%. Even under optimistic assumptions (perfect step-level death prediction), a residual gap of >10pp between strategies is expected, as fatal trajectories involve cumulative hallucination errors preceding the death event."

### SF4 [R19-W2]: Conclusion 扩展 ~0.5 页

围绕 framework-centric narrative：
1. Key findings summary（3 句：framework enables signal decomposition → death avoidance dominates → robust across interventions）
2. Diagnostic value：框架可提前判断 routing 是否值得投入，避免无效 router 训练投资
3. Framework applicability：需要 episode-level evaluation + catastrophic failure 存在的环境
4. Future directions：NetHack replication, step-level signal extraction (death-proximal labeling), mixed-strategy analysis, extension to multi-agent settings

### SF5 [R13-W6]: §4 Safe RL 扩展 5+ 句

扩展为：
"Death avoidance as an implicit routing signal connects to safe RL. Constrained MDPs (Altman, 1999) formalize safety through state or cost constraints; our death-avoidance mechanism can be viewed as an implicit absorbing-state constraint, where routing to deterministic computation avoids irreversible penalty states. Risk-sensitive RL (Mihatsch & Neuneier, 2002) optimizes CVaR-type objectives to minimize catastrophic outcomes—our framework diagnoses when and why such risk-sensitivity emerges in routing, rather than prescribing it. Unlike explicit safe RL methods that require constraint specification, our routing mechanism achieves safety implicitly through computation mode selection, without the agent being aware of the safety objective. Ruan et al. (2024) provide a taxonomy of LM agent failures identifying catastrophic actions as a distinct category, consistent with our finding that death events dominate routing signal."

补 bib：altman1999constrained, mihatsch2002risk, natarajan2013learning（noisy label learning，与 MF5 §3.6 关联）

与 MF1 和 MF3 统一处理。Abstract 结构：
- 句1-2：问题+框架（"Per-step oracle comparison degenerates to ties in sparse-reward environments. We propose episode-level counterfactual diagnosis..."）
- 句3-4：key finding（"Applying this framework to ScienceWorld reveals that 78.9% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry..."）
- 句5-6：validation（"Cross-model replication, four intervention axes, and boundary probes confirm..."）
- 句7：implication（"Our framework diagnoses when routing adds value..."）
- CI, δ, P sensitivity 全部移到正文

### SF7 [R18-M1]: Modality → computation mode 验证

全文搜索 "modality"（在 internal/deterministic 语境下）。R18 已做全局替换，确认是否遗漏。如有遗漏补上。

§3.3 改为：
"The key decline (N=1→N=3, Δ=-13.5pp, z=2.75, p=0.003) is well-powered: with N=200 paired episodes, MDE ≈ 8pp at α=0.05, 80% power. The plateau claim (N=3→N=5, +1.0pp) is also supported: if a ≥8pp improvement existed, we would detect it; the observed +1.0pp falls within noise. We characterize this as suggestive evidence of a Reflexion ceiling rather than a conclusive demonstration."

### SF9 [NEW]: §5 Routing Necessity 新段

§5 新增 "Practical Value of Routing" 段（~4-5 句）：
"Our framework reveals a striking implication: 96.3% of oracle routing decisions favor always-deterministic, and 62% of episodes show no strategy preference. Routing, in its current form, is nearly unnecessary on ScienceWorld. This is not a limitation but a diagnostic finding—the framework's value lies precisely in making such assessments possible before investing in router training. Without counterfactual diagnosis, the default assumption (diverse routing signals exist) would lead to wasted computation on router architectures that cannot outperform the trivial always-deterministic baseline. The framework saves this investment by quantifying routing signal composition upfront."

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FzB9SQtP6NsCVW2nTrhnQf`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Large language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.
4	A Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent navigating ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation with 30 task types, faces this choice at every step. Tracking temperature thresholds, container states, and multi-step procedures via internal reasoning is error-prone: the model may hallucinate that water has boiled when the stove was never activated, or that an object has been moved when the action was invalid. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The practical question for agent design is therefore: \emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?}
5	
6	Existing routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision.
7	Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} teaches models to insert API calls when doing so reduces perplexity; ART \cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} selects among heterogeneous modules (search, code, vision) per subtask.
8	More recent adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \cite{mtrouter-2026}.
9	Concurrent analyses of when and whether to invoke tools~\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026} further presuppose diverse routing signals.
10	The shared assumption is that routing signal stems from diverse sources (accuracy, latency, capability matching) and that a learned router can exploit this signal to improve over any fixed strategy.
11	But no prior work has systematically decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments. Without such a decomposition, the field cannot distinguish settings where diverse routing signals justify learned routers from those where a single failure mode dominates and a fixed strategy suffices.
12	
13	We propose a \emph{formal diagnostic framework} for answering this question. The framework comprises (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), a penalty-dependent metric that decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\S\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \textbf{78.9\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding that holds regardless of how death is penalized. Under ScienceWorld's specific penalty structure ($P{=}100$), the penalty-weighted DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$), consistent with the predicted value from observed $\delta{=}0.265$ and $P{=}100$.
14	The internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic agent death (a $-100$ penalty), while the deterministic strategy survives the same episodes.
15	Once death episodes are removed, the performance gap collapses to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$. A per-task consistency check ($r = 0.97$) confirms that the near-perfect correlation is a mathematical consequence of death dominance when $P$ is constant (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}).
16	
17	This framework addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\% of ScienceWorld steps produce identical scores regardless of computation mode; \S\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\S\ref{sec:baselines}). Our counterfactual approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, then compares episode outcomes. The framework applies to any environment with controllable computation mode comparison and binary catastrophic outcomes.
18	
19	Two cross-context checks probe the specificity of this finding.
20	First, a MATH Level~4--5 evaluation ($N{=}795$ problems, code interpreter as deterministic computation mode) confirms no routing signal without catastrophic failure: internal reasoning and code interpretation achieve near-identical accuracy ($73.5\%$ vs.\ $73.0\%$, $p = 0.81$).
21	Second, a cross-model replication with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (11 task types, $N{=}220$) shows death dominance replicates across model families with direction consistency 10/10 ($+30.0$~pp vs.\ $+26.5$~pp for Qwen; \S\ref{sec:cross-model}).
22	Four intervention axes test whether death dominance is fragile: retry, Reflexion~\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\times}$ parameter scaling (72B), and action-space restriction all reduce death rates, yet none closes the gap to deterministic safety (\S\ref{sec:agent-improvement}). Action filtering (blocking the fatal action during exploration) and 72B scaling yield the strongest reductions yet still leave ${\geq}$12~pp residual gaps, results not predictable from the penalty structure alone.
23	An exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
24	
25	Our contributions are:
26	Our contributions: (1)~an \textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} with dual penalty-independent and penalty-dependent metrics (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:daf}, \S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}); (2)~\textbf{evidence that death dominance resists interventions}: 78.9\% of non-tie labels arise from death asymmetry, and four axes (retry, self-correction, $10{\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) fail to close the gap (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\textbf{cross-environment boundary probes}: MATH ($p = 0.81$) confirms the null, ALFWorld identifies information asymmetry, and Llama-3.1-8B replication confirms cross-model consistency (\S\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\ref{sec:cross-model}).
27	
28	
29

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}
5	The router diagnostic (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}
8	While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\S\ref{sec:daf}), three properties were not predictable a priori: (i)~\emph{routing prior}---the oracle matches always-deterministic on 96.3\% of decisions (2.89-point improvement), and threshold routing with perfect death risk knowledge still cannot outperform always-deterministic (Appendix~\ref{sec:threshold-routing}), so the rational prior is ``do not route''; (ii)~\emph{intervention resistance}---retry ($43.5\%$), Reflexion ($34.0\%$), $10{\times}$ scaling ($30.6\%$), and action-space filtering ($29.0\%$) all leave ${\geq}$12~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety ($16.3\%$), with Reflexion and scaling converging to overlapping Wilson CIs suggesting a shared bound from lacking ground-truth state access; (iii)~\emph{cross-model stability}---Qwen-7B and Llama-8B show nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\sim}$42\%), and 72B scaling leaves death dominance intact (DAF $= 92.4\%$). More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.
9	
10	\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}
11	All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to misuse of \texttt{focus on [object]}, the answer-submission action, as a general interaction command (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}). The root cause is grounding failure: agents parse task descriptions correctly but cannot map understanding to valid primitives; the majority of death episodes show effective reasoning in non-fatal steps before a single grounding error triggers irreversible failure. The internal strategy's higher death count (105 vs.\ 40) reflects more frequent entry into catastrophic states, not different within-episode behavior (${\sim}5\%$ \texttt{focus on} rate in both); lethality depends jointly on misuse rate and answer distribution (\texttt{find-non-living-thing} has zero deaths despite high submission rate because non-living objects are the majority class). Direct grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate vulnerability ($48.0\% \to 29.0\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
14	A three-stage diagnostic (${\sim}$50-episode pilot $\to$ death-rate differential $\to$ death-label fraction; Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}) correctly predicts outcomes for all four conditions (Qwen-7B, Llama-8B, Qwen-72B, MATH). Our router training consumed ${\sim}$200 GPU-hours without exceeding always-deterministic; this diagnostic could have predicted that from a 50-episode pilot. The 4B router's end-to-end score ($-12.03$, below always-deterministic $-9.47$) may partly reflect distribution shift at deployment~\citep{dagger-2011}.
15	
16	\paragraph{Framework applicability.}
17	The penalty-independent label fraction (78.9\%) is the primary diagnostic; DAF increases monotonically with $P$ (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Preliminary pilots on MiniHack~\citep{minihack-2021} (0\% death) and Crafter~\citep{crafter-2022} (100\% death, both strategies) produced no death asymmetry, illustrating two boundary conditions: no death mechanism yields no signal; universal death lacks exploitable asymmetry. NetHack~\citep{nethack-2020} combat tasks remain the most direct replication target \citep{balrog-2025,netplay-2024}.
18	
```

```text
0	\section{Experiments}
1	
2	We analyze 11 of ScienceWorld's 30 task types (245 episodes); the 19 excluded types (63\%) lack actionable routing signal due to development contamination (5), taxonomic redundancy (7), or agent-floor performance (7). This scope limits generalizability: conclusions apply to the included subset, not to all ScienceWorld tasks. An anti-cherry-picking analysis confirms that death dominance holds in 18 of these 19 types, with a mean $\Delta\text{DR} = +38.2$\,pp exceeding the included tasks' $+28.7$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:per-task}).
3	
4	\subsection{Death Avoidance Decomposition}
5	\label{sec:death-decomp}
6	
7	\begin{figure}[t]
8	\centering
9	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_daf_scatter.pdf}
10	\caption{Per-task scatter: death rate differential (internal $-$ deterministic) vs.\ performance gap ($\Delta_{\text{full}}$). $r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$, $n = 11$ task types. Since $\Delta_{\text{full}} \approx \delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}}$ with $P$ constant, $r \to 1$ as DAF $\to 1$; the near-perfect correlation is a structural consequence of death dominance, not an independent finding (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}).}
11	\label{fig:decomposition}
12	\end{figure}
13	
14	Of 95 non-tie routing labels across 245 episodes, 75 (78.9\%) arise from death asymmetry: one strategy dies while the other survives. This label fraction is penalty-independent---it depends only on which episodes produce asymmetric deaths, not on how death is penalized. Of these 75, 54 are sole-signal labels (episodes where only one strategy produced a non-zero score): both strategies score zero without death, so death avoidance was the \emph{sole} discriminating signal. The remaining 21 death-asymmetry labels involve episodes where the surviving strategy also earned positive score. At $P{=}0$, the 54 sole-signal labels reduce to ties, leaving 41 non-tie labels of which 21 (51.2\%) still involve death asymmetry; the labeling function itself is penalty-independent (Algorithm~\ref{alg:labeling}), but the practical routing value of death avoidance requires $P > 0$.
15	
16	Under ScienceWorld's penalty structure ($P{=}100$), the full score gap is $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$; excluding death episodes, $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$, yielding DAF $= 90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$, episode-level bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$). DAF varies with penalty magnitude ($50.4\%$ at $P{=}10$; $70.8\%$ at $P{=}25$; $82.6\%$ at $P{=}50$; $90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}), confirming that the 78.9\% label fraction provides the more robust, penalty-independent characterization. Given $\delta = 0.265$ and $P = 100$, the predicted DAF $= \delta P / (\delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}}) = 26.5 / 29.37 \approx 90.3\%$, closely matching the observed value; DAF is thus a derived quantity once $\delta$, $P$, and $\Delta_{\text{nd}}$ are known.
17	
18	\paragraph{Per-task correlation structure.}
19	The internal strategy dies in 42.9\% of episodes versus 16.3\% for deterministic (2.6$\times$), consistent with the internal strategy's reliance on self-generated observations that compound prediction errors across steps. Per-task, death rate differential correlates with $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\ref{fig:decomposition}). Since $P$ is constant across all task types, $\Delta_{\text{full}} = P \cdot \delta + \Delta_{\text{nd}}$ makes $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ a linear function of $\delta$ with residual $\Delta_{\text{nd}}$; when DAF $\rightarrow 1$, this residual vanishes and $r \rightarrow 1$ by construction. The near-perfect correlation is therefore a mathematical consequence of death dominance, not an independent empirical finding. We retain it as an internal consistency check. The correlation drops below significance at $P \leq 25$ (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Of 245 episodes, 86 are \texttt{det\_preferred}, 9 \texttt{internal\_preferred}, and 150 \texttt{no\_preference}.
20	
21	\paragraph{Signal concentration and binary structure.}
22	The Signal Concentration Index (SCI $= 0.2147$; Appendix~\ref{app:sci}) confirms low concentration: the top-3 task types account for only $39.8\%$ of total death-rate differential. The aggregate 78.9\% reflects a per-task binary structure: all 10 death-prone task types produce 100\% death-asymmetry labels, while \texttt{find-non-living-thing} (zero deaths, $\Delta = +9.58$ from non-death scores) produces 0\%. The framework thus diagnoses a binary property (``is this environment death-dominated?'') rather than a continuous routing signal. Removing \texttt{find-non-living-thing} raises the label fraction to 100\% and DAF to 96.8\% (Appendix~\ref{app:loto-daf}).
23	
24	\paragraph{Sensitivity and seed robustness.}
25	Episode-level bootstrap ($B = 10{,}000$) yields 95\% CI $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$ for DAF with Cohen's $d = 3.02$, indicating a large effect size; leave-one-task-out analysis confirms stability (DAF range: 75.9\%--78.9\%; Appendix~\ref{app:loto-daf}). An independent seed replication ($N{=}220$) yields DAF $= 89.7\%$, within 0.7~pp of the primary analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}). As a conservative sensitivity analysis, cluster-robust inference treating the $K{=}11$ task types as clusters ($p < 0.001$) confirms significance even under within-cluster correlation; 28-task directional consistency (100\%) provides further support (Appendices~\ref{app:wild-cluster}--\ref{app:per-task-ci}).
26	
27	\subsection{Multi-Model Validation}
28	\label{sec:cross-model}
29

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012WJjw8dg5EVmjS8cvUBoWk`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Tool Use and External Computation in LLMs.}
4	A growing body of work augments language models with external tools \citep{tool-learning-survey-2024}, spanning several functional categories.
5	\emph{Self-supervised} approaches learn tool invocation from loss signals: Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} inserts API calls filtered by perplexity reduction; ToolACE \cite{toolace-2025} curates synthetic data for function-calling accuracy.
6	\emph{Token-level} methods embed tools into the model vocabulary: ToolkenGPT \cite{toolkengpt-2023} represents tools as special tokens resolved at inference.
7	\emph{Task-level} decomposition routes subtasks to specialized modules: ART \cite{art-2023} generates tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} composes heterogeneous modules per subtask.
8	\emph{RL-based} methods learn routing policies from reward: ReTool \cite{retool-2025} discovers when code execution is preferable to chain-of-thought.
9	All focus on \emph{learning to use tools}; we ask a prior question: \emph{what signal drives the routing decision?}
10	Recent concurrent work sharpens this question: \citet{tool-overuse-illusion-2026} identify a cognitive-illusion effect where LLMs default to external tools even when internal knowledge suffices, finding that tool overuse correlates with model uncertainty rather than task difficulty. \citet{when2tool-2026} show tool-call decisions are encoded in hidden states before reasoning, \citet{tool-use-tax-2026} quantify a ``tool-use tax,'' and \citet{to-call-or-not-2026} propose a necessity, utility, and affordability framework. Our decomposition provides environment-grounded evidence: in ScienceWorld, 78.9\% of routing labels trace to catastrophic-failure prevention.
11	
12	\paragraph{Adaptive Computation and Routing.}
13	Adaptive routing traces back to learned halting policies \citep{graves2016act}. In the LLM era, CogRouter \cite{cogrouter-2026} adapts reasoning depth per step, ARES \cite{ares-2026} selects among reasoning effort levels, Mixture-of-Recursions \cite{mixture-of-recursions-2025} learns token-level recursion depths, and MTRouter \cite{mtrouter-2026} addresses multi-turn routing.
14	RouteLLM \citep{routellm-2024} learns routers from preference data to select between strong and weak models.
15	These optimize \emph{how} to route; we reveal \emph{why} routing helps: in interactive environments, the dominant signal is catastrophic-failure avoidance.
16	
17	\paragraph{Counterfactual Evaluation in RL.}
18	Prior work generates counterfactual explanations for RL agents by perturbing individual actions within a fixed policy \citep{olson2021counterfactual, gajcin2023counterfactual, amitai2024explaining}.
19	Our approach differs in both purpose and granularity: we execute two complete episodes under different routing strategies, producing prospective paired comparisons that isolate routing signal \emph{before} any router is trained.
20	
21	\paragraph{LLM Agent Environments and Reasoning.}
22	ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022} serves as our primary testbed; Inner Monologue \cite{huang2022inner} grounds LLM planning in embodied feedback; Voyager \cite{wang2023voyager} demonstrates open-ended LLM agents; ALFWorld \cite{alfworld-2021} provides a complementary household environment; AgentBench \cite{agentbench-2024} offers systematic evaluation. \citet{zhou2024webarena} document common failure modes (premature termination, hallucinated actions) in web-based agents, consistent with our finding that state hallucination is the primary internal-strategy failure mechanism. ReAct \cite{react-2023} serves as our internal baseline; Reflexion \cite{reflexion-2023} extends this with verbal self-reflection; LATS \cite{lats-2024} adds tree search. SelfCheckGPT \cite{selfcheckgpt-2023} detects unreliable LLM outputs; our work shows that in interactive environments, such unreliability manifests as catastrophic death.
23	
24	\paragraph{Safe RL.}
25	Safe RL methods \citep{garcia2015comprehensive,tamar2015cvar,achiam2017cpo,crpo-2021,moldovan2012safe} enforce constraints in MDPs via risk-sensitive objectives, constrained optimization, or safe exploration; \citet{ruan2024failures} provide a taxonomy of LM agent failures identifying catastrophic actions as a distinct category; SafeRoute \citep{saferoute-2025} applies adaptive model selection for safety guardrails. These assume the agent can learn to avoid catastrophic states; our results show that computation mode routing already functions as an implicit safety mechanism---the deterministic strategy sidesteps catastrophic states entirely.
26	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01H5Tv8iBwsfCfAQwyHBkqQx`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet no systematic methodology exists for diagnosing \emph{what} drives routing decisions. We introduce an episode-level counterfactual framework that decomposes routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Applied to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), \textbf{78.9\% of non-tie routing labels} arise from death asymmetry: the internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic agent death, while the deterministic strategy survives. This death dominance resists four representative intervention axes spanning retry, self-correction, $10{\times}$ parameter scaling, and action-space restriction; all reduce death rates yet none closes the gap to deterministic safety. A MATH evaluation ($N{=}795$, $p = 0.81$) confirms no routing signal without catastrophic failure. The framework reveals when catastrophic failure makes routing degenerate and when learned routers may add value.
3	\end{abstract}
4	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QdvxcJrAzNp7THMPoggawn`</summary>

```text
45	\caption{Counterfactual episode collection and analysis pipeline. Each task instance is run under both pure strategies independently, producing episode-level labels via Algorithm~\ref{alg:labeling}.}
46	\label{fig:pipeline}
47	\end{figure}
48	
49	\subsection{Death Avoidance Factor}
50	\label{sec:daf}
51	
52	To quantify the contribution of death avoidance to routing signal, we decompose the mean score gap between strategies. Let $\delta = \mathbb{E}[d^{\tt int}] - \mathbb{E}[d^{\tt det}]$ denote the death-rate differential and $\Delta_{\text{nd}}$ the mean score gap restricted to episodes where neither strategy dies. Partitioning episodes by death status, death-asymmetric episodes (where only one strategy dies) contribute a per-episode gap of $P + \bar{s}_{\text{surv}}$, where $\bar{s}_{\text{surv}}$ is the surviving strategy's mean score; when $\bar{s}_{\text{surv}} \ll P$ (in our data, $\bar{s}_{\text{surv}} = 0.87$, or $0.9\%$ of $P$), these episodes contribute ${\approx}\,\delta P$ in aggregate. The full gap thus decomposes as:
53	\begin{equation}
54	    \Delta_{\text{full}} \approx \underbrace{\delta \cdot P}_{\text{death contribution}} + \Delta_{\text{nd}}
55	\end{equation}
56	The approximation residual is $29.60 - 29.37 = 0.23$ ($0.8\%$ of $\Delta_{\text{full}}$).
57	
58	We define the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) as the fraction of the full score gap attributable to death avoidance:
59	\begin{equation}
60	    \text{DAF} = 1 - \frac{\Delta_{\text{nd}}}{\Delta_{\text{full}}} \approx \frac{\delta P}{\delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}}}
61	\end{equation}%
62	\footnote{When $\Delta_{\text{nd}} < 0$ (deterministic strategy scores lower than internal on non-death episodes), DAF exceeds 1; death avoidance then overcompensates for a non-death disadvantage.}
63	
64	Since DAF $= \delta P / (\delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}})$, it increases monotonically with $P$, a mathematical consequence rather than an empirical finding. We therefore report both DAF at the environment default ($P{=}100$) and the penalty-independent death-asymmetry label fraction as complementary metrics.
65	
66	\paragraph{Statistical inference.}
67	We quantify uncertainty via episode-level bootstrap resampling ($B = 10{,}000$). Each resample draws $N = 245$ episodes with replacement, recomputes all metrics, and derives percentile-based 95\% CIs. We resample at the episode level (not the step level) because the episode is the unit of counterfactual comparison. Penalty sensitivity analysis across hypothetical $P$ values is in Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}.
68	
69	We also measure whether death-avoidance signal is concentrated in a few task types via a Signal Concentration Index (SCI $\in [0, 1]$; Appendix~\ref{app:sci}), reported in \S\ref{sec:death-decomp}.
70	
71	\paragraph{Scope of episode-level analysis.}
72	At step level, 97.4\% of oracle comparisons yield ties (\S\ref{sec:collection}); of 2{,}040 training steps, only 0.3\% exhibit strict directional conflict with episode labels, with 60.3\% pre-event neutral (neither strategy advantaged). Episode labels thus apply correct direction to uninformative steps---a granularity mismatch, not label error---explaining why step-level router training approaches chance performance (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
73	
```

55	\end{tabular}
56	\caption{Internal-strategy death rates under agent improvement methods. Top: all 11 tasks (72B: independently collected at both internal and deterministic). Bottom: 10 death-prone tasks (\texttt{find-non-living-thing} excluded, 0\% death at all scales). All interventions reduce death rate but none approaches deterministic-level safety.}
57	\label{tab:agent-improvement}
58	\end{table}
59	
60	We test whether agent-level improvement reduces death dominance (Table~\ref{tab:agent-improvement}). Retry ($N{=}5$ attempts without self-correction) yields marginal reduction on death-prone tasks ($43.5\%$ vs.\ $48.0\%$ baseline, $\Delta = -4.5$~pp). Reflexion~\citep{reflexion-2023} (verbal self-reflection between trials) reduces death to 31.4\% across all 11 tasks by rescuing 29.3\% of first-trial deaths, but the gap to deterministic remains 15.1~pp; on the 10 death-prone tasks, Reflexion achieves ${\sim}$35\%. Under Reflexion ($N{=}3$, matched-episode analysis), $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = -0.47$, yielding DAF $> 100\%$: the deterministic advantage is entirely attributable to death avoidance, and in non-death episodes the self-correcting agent slightly outperforms the deterministic baseline. Scaling to Qwen2.5-72B ($10{\times}$ parameters; both internal and deterministic strategies independently collected at 72B, 255 episodes across all 11 task types) reduces death rate to $30.6\%$. The 72B deterministic strategy achieves $2.75\%$ death rate ($\Delta\text{DR} = +27.8$~pp), and $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.28$, yielding DAF $= 92.4\%$ (7B: $90.4\%$); scaling lowers death rate but leaves death dominance intact.
61	
62	\paragraph{Dose-response analysis.}
63	We extend both interventions to $N{=}5$ trials on death-prone tasks ($n{=}200$; Figure~\ref{fig:dose-response}). Retry is nearly flat ($48.0\% \to 43.5\%$, $\Delta = -4.5$~pp over five trials). Reflexion drops steeply to $34.0\%$ at $N{=}3$ ($\Delta = -13.5$~pp, $z = 2.75$, $p = 0.003$) then plateaus ($34$--$36.5\%$ for $N \geq 3$, pairwise $p > 0.5$); the minimum detectable effect at $n{=}200$ is ${\sim}14$~pp ($\alpha = 0.05$, 80\% power), indicating adequate sensitivity for the observed $N{=}1 \to 3$ reduction but not for smaller plateau-region changes. Retry's per-trial conditional death rate stays constant at ${\sim}48\%$; Reflexion's declines to ${\sim}31\%$ by Trial~3 (per-task breakdown in Appendix~\ref{app:dose-response}).
64	
65	\begin{figure}[t]
66	\centering
67	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/dose_response.pdf}
68	\caption{Death rate as a function of trial count ($N$) for Retry and Reflexion on 10 death-prone tasks ($n{=}200$). Retry (blue) is nearly flat; Reflexion (red) drops steeply through $N{=}3$ then plateaus. Horizontal lines: 72B scaling baseline ($30.6\%$, all 11 tasks) and deterministic baseline ($16.3\%$). Error bands: Wilson 95\% CIs.}
69	\label{fig:dose-response}
70	\end{figure}
71	
72	\subsection{Cross-Environment Boundary Probes}
73	\label{sec:math-contrast}
74	\label{sec:cross-env}
75	
76	We evaluate two additional environments as boundary probes (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env-details}). ``Deterministic'' denotes qualitatively different computation modes per environment: ScienceWorld uses an oracle simulator with ground-truth state, MATH uses a Python code interpreter, and ALFWorld uses admissible-action enumeration. These are not equivalent; results probe framework applicability rather than replicate findings.
77	
78	\paragraph{MATH.} On MATH Level~4--5~\citep{math-2021} ($N{=}795$, code interpreter as deterministic computation mode), internal reasoning and code interpretation achieve near-identical accuracy ($73.5\%$ vs.\ $73.0\%$, McNemar $p = 0.81$, 95\% CI $[-2.6, +3.6]$~pp), with balanced labels (107 vs.\ 95, 74.6\% no-preference). Without catastrophic failure, no routing signal emerges.
79	
80	\paragraph{ALFWorld.} ALFWorld~\citep{alfworld-2021} (6 task types, 135 games) provides the deterministic strategy with admissible actions, an information asymmetry rather than a computation mode switch. The success rate gap is $27.4\%$ vs.\ $3.0\%$ (35 det-preferred, 2 int-preferred, 98 no-preference). Of 37 non-tie labels, 35 (94.6\%) favor the information-advantaged strategy, a one-directional dominance analogous to ScienceWorld's death dominance but driven by information asymmetry rather than catastrophic failure. The framework diagnoses this qualitatively different mechanism without modification.
81	
82	\subsection{Baselines}
83	\label{sec:baselines}
84	
85	\begin{table}[t]
86	\centering
87	\small
88	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
89	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
90	\toprule
91	Strategy & Score & 95\% CI & Death \% \\
92	\midrule
93	Always-Internal & $-39.07$ & [$-46.40$, $-32.27$] & 42.9 \\
94	Always-Deterministic & $-9.47$ & [$-14.71$, $-4.29$] & 16.3 \\
95	Random & $-22.38$ & [$-28.67$, $-15.75$] & 27.8 \\
96	Oracle & $-6.58$ & [$-11.54$, $-1.70$] & 14.3 \\
97	\midrule
98	Trained Router$^\dagger$ & $-10.70$ & [$-16.33$, $-5.27$] & 17.6 \\
99	\bottomrule
100	\end{tabular}}
101	\caption{Episode-weighted mean scores on ScienceWorld (11 tasks, 245 episodes). $\dagger$\,Single random seed (42) per episode pair; see Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness} for 8-seed robustness analysis.}
102	\label{tab:baselines}
103	\end{table}
104	
105	Always-deterministic ($-9.47$) outperforms always-internal ($-39.07$) by 29.60 points. The oracle improves only to $-6.58$, with 96.3\% of decisions matching always-deterministic---an \emph{oracle degeneracy} where an always-deterministic lookup achieves 90.5\% of non-tie oracle decisions (balanced accuracy 50.0\%: perfect deterministic recall, zero internal recall).
106	
107	\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic Probe}
108	\label{sec:router-diagnostic}
109	
110	We probe whether counterfactual labels can train a step-level router as a diagnostic of label informativeness. The oracle margin (2.89 points) caps routing upside; at step level, of 2{,}040 training steps only 0.3\% exhibit strict directional conflict (step-level oracle opposite to episode label) and 60.3\% are pre-event neutral (neither strategy advantaged)---a granularity mismatch, not label error---leaving minimal signal for the router. An implicit router (Qwen3, LoRA; Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}) trained at three scales (0.6B, 1.7B, 4B) with 4-seed configurations shows flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\%$, within seed variance). At episode level, only 4B exceeds always-deterministic ($59.0 \pm 1.9\%$, $p < 0.005$; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}); 0.6B and 1.7B remain at or below chance. The multi-step nature of routing (10--50 actions with compounding state uncertainty) creates inherent label noise between step-level decisions and episode-level outcomes. Training and validation use episode-level splits (Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}), ensuring no within-episode information leakage.
111	
112	\paragraph{End-to-end deployment.} The 4B router deployed end-to-end ($N{=}245$) scores $-12.03$ (95\% CI: $[-17.82, -6.45]$), not exceeding always-deterministic ($-9.47$). Paraphrase-based augmentation ($25\%$--$800\%$) provides no improvement; the $3.7\%$ internal-preferred base rate makes real-data collection expensive (Appendices~\ref{app:seed-robustness}--\ref{app:ablations}).
113

Starting with the Abstract rewrite—I'm restructuring it around the framework concept, leading with the sparse reward degeneracy problem in ScienceWorld, then introducing the episode-level counterfactual approach that separates death-avoidance from non-death routing signals, followed by the empirical validation across task types and episodes, and concluding with the implications for oracle-based evaluation.

Now moving to the Introduction contributions section...

The framework overcomes the per-step oracle degeneracy problem through episode-level counterfactual analysis, enabling meaningful signal decomposition in sparse-reward settings. Applying this to ScienceWorld reveals that death asymmetry accounts for nearly 79% of non-tie routing labels—a single dominant mechanism. Cross-model validation with Llama-3.1-8B, multiple intervention strategies, and boundary testing across different environments confirm both the framework's diagnostic power and the robustness of this finding. (\S\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\ref{sec:cross-model}).

Now for the non-triviality section, I need to expand substantially. The key insight is that death dominance isn't just a mathematical artifact—it's surprising because prior routing work assumes diverse signals drive routing decisions, yet we're finding a single mechanism explains most of the behavior. I'm also showing that intuitive interventions like scaling or self-correction fail to eliminate this dominance, which contradicts what the experimental setup might suggest. And across different model sizes, the pattern holds remarkably consistent.

The precise quantification matters too—being able to say 78.9% instead of "most" enables actual deployment decisions. That's the real contribution here: not just identifying the phenomenon, but measuring it rigorously enough to be actionable.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "old_string": "\\begin{abstract}\nModular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet no systematic methodology exists for diagnosing \\emph{what} drives routing decisions. We introduce an episode-level counterfactual framework that decomposes routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Applied to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} arise from death asymmetry: the internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic agent death, while the deterministic strategy survives. This death dominance resists four representative intervention axes spanning retry, self-correction, $10{\\times}$ parameter scaling, and action-space restriction; all reduce death rates yet none closes the gap to deterministic safety. A MATH evaluation ($N{=}795$, $p = 0.81$) confirms no routing signal without catastrophic failure. The framework reveals when catastrophic failure makes routing degenerate and when learned routers may add value.\n\\end{abstract}",
  "new_string": "\\begin{abstract}\nPer-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards---in ScienceWorld, 97.4\\% of steps yield identical scores regardless of computation mode. We introduce an episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework that overcomes this degeneracy by decomposing routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components via paired pure-strategy episodes. Applying this framework to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a penalty-independent result; a derived measure (DAF) reaches 90.4\\% at $P{=}100$. Cross-model replication (Llama-3.1-8B), four intervention axes (retry, self-correction, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction), and boundary probes against contrasting environments (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of this finding. The framework diagnoses when catastrophic failure makes routing signal degenerate, enabling practitioners to assess routing value before investing in router training.\n\\end{abstract}"
}
```

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "af006076-76a5-414c-a519-8f90a727d0f4",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "ff5a0a43-04c5-4143-bf0d-c9aa2172b397",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "671ca5ca-28b5-459e-aba0-aba159108d10",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "Our contributions are:\nOur contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} with dual penalty-independent and penalty-dependent metrics (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}, \\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}); (2)~\\textbf{evidence that death dominance resists interventions}: 78.9\\% of non-tie labels arise from death asymmetry, and four axes (retry, self-correction, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) fail to close the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-environment boundary probes}: MATH ($p = 0.81$) confirms the null, ALFWorld identifies information asymmetry, and Llama-3.1-8B replication confirms cross-model consistency (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).",
    "newString": "Our contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate) in sparse-reward environments, enabling routing signal decomposition (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}); (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism dominates routing signal (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-model validation} (Llama-3.1-8B), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the finding (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nLarge language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \\emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.\nA Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent navigating ScienceWorld \\cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation with 30 task types, faces this choice at every step. Tracking temperature thresholds, container states, and multi-step procedures via internal reasoning is error-prone: the model may hallucinate that water has boiled when the stove was never activated, or that an object has been moved when the action was invalid. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The practical question for agent design is therefore: \\emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?}\n\nExisting routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision.\nToolformer \\cite{toolformer-2023} teaches models to insert API calls when doing so reduces perplexity; ART \\cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \\cite{chameleon-2023} selects among heterogeneous modules (search, code, vision) per subtask.\nMore recent adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \\cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \\cite{mtrouter-2026}.\nConcurrent analyses of when and whether to invoke tools~\\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026} further presuppose diverse routing signals.\nThe shared assumption is that routing signal stems from diverse sources (accuracy, latency, capability matching) and that a learned router can exploit this signal to improve over any fixed strategy.\nBut no prior work has systematically decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments. Without such a decomposition, the field cannot distinguish settings where diverse routing signals justify learned routers from those where a single failure mode dominates and a fixed strategy suffices.\n\nWe propose a \\emph{formal diagnostic framework} for answering this question. The framework comprises (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), a penalty-dependent metric that decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding that holds regardless of how death is penalized. Under ScienceWorld's specific penalty structure ($P{=}100$), the penalty-weighted DAF reaches $90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$), consistent with the predicted value from observed $\\delta{=}0.265$ and $P{=}100$.\nThe internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic agent death (a $-100$ penalty), while the deterministic strategy survives the same episodes.\nOnce death episodes are removed, the performance gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$. A per-task consistency check ($r = 0.97$) confirms that the near-perfect correlation is a mathematical consequence of death dominance when $P$ is constant (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}).\n\nThis framework addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\\% of ScienceWorld steps produce identical scores regardless of computation mode; \\S\\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}). Our counterfactual approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, then compares episode outcomes. The framework applies to any environment with controllable computation mode comparison and binary catastrophic outcomes.\n\nTwo cross-context checks probe the specificity of this finding.\nFirst, a MATH Level~4--5 evaluation ($N{=}795$ problems, code interpreter as deterministic computation mode) confirms no routing signal without catastrophic failure: internal reasoning and code interpretation achieve near-identical accuracy ($73.5\\%$ vs.\\ $73.0\\%$, $p = 0.81$).\nSecond, a cross-model replication with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (11 task types, $N{=}220$) shows death dominance replicates across model families with direction consistency 10/10 ($+30.0$~pp vs.\\ $+26.5$~pp for Qwen; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\nFour intervention axes test whether death dominance is fragile: retry, Reflexion~\\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\\times}$ parameter scaling (72B), and action-space restriction all reduce death rates, yet none closes the gap to deterministic safety (\\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}). Action filtering (blocking the fatal action during exploration) and 72B scaling yield the strongest reductions yet still leave ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps, results not predictable from the penalty structure alone.\nAn exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).\n\nOur contributions are:\nOur contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} with dual penalty-independent and penalty-dependent metrics (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}, \\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}); (2)~\\textbf{evidence that death dominance resists interventions}: 78.9\\% of non-tie labels arise from death asymmetry, and four axes (retry, self-correction, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) fail to close the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-environment boundary probes}: MATH ($p = 0.81$) confirms the null, ALFWorld identifies information asymmetry, and Llama-3.1-8B replication confirms cross-model consistency (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\n\n",

More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}\nWhile death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), its practical implications were not predictable a priori.\n\\emph{Routing literature assumptions.}\nExisting routing work (Toolformer, CogRouter, ART, When2Tool, RouteLLM) implicitly assumes diverse routing signals---capability matching, efficiency, task complexity. No prior work has quantified signal composition; our finding that a single mechanism accounts for 78.9\\% of routing labels contradicts this implicit diversity assumption.\n\\emph{Intervention resistance.}\nFrom the setup alone ($P{=}100$, internal DR${}=42.9\\%$), one might predict that scaling, self-correction, or action filtering would substantially reduce death vulnerability. Instead, all four axes fail to eliminate death dominance: 72B DAF${}= 92.4\\%$; Reflexion DAF${}>100\\%$ ($\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = -0.47$, meaning self-correction improves non-death performance while death dominance remains intact); action filtering reduces DR to 29\\% but ${\\geq}12$~pp residual gap persists. The oracle matches always-deterministic on 96.3\\% of decisions, and threshold routing with perfect death risk knowledge still cannot outperform always-deterministic (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}).\n\\emph{Cross-model stability.}\nQwen-7B ($+26.5$~pp) and Llama-8B ($+30.0$~pp) show nearly identical death-rate differentials despite different architectures; internal death rates converge to ${\\sim}42\\%$, suggesting death vulnerability is a property of the task-environment interaction, not model-specific weakness.\nEven granting predictability in hindsight, precise quantification (78.9\\%, not ``most'') enables principled deployment decisions---and the framework that produces this quantification is the primary contribution. More aggressive interventions (Tree-of-Thought, process reward models) remain untested; we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound."
}
